# max4 round64: inherited-parameter secondary-dual pattern report

**Completed numerical discovery; no new fact; original minimax OPEN.** All six
authorized jobs completed: calibration, four full secondary-dual solves, and
one independently coded H4 readback. All five solver statuses were `optimal`.
The canonical ledger is `closed_exhausted`:180 accounted seconds,
41.86519651800336 measured external child seconds, five solver invocations,
zero remaining slots. No correction, failed job, timeout, retry, or fallback.

The useful pattern is asymmetric. The right child's finite multipliers nearly
reproduce the previous inherited child's finite multipliers, while its optimum
attachments have a different scale, near0.6316. The left child changes
substantially, with large backward coefficients near dyadic endpoints. At H6
there are also small, clearly resolved forward cross-block coefficients.
These observations suggest a richer recursive law; they do not establish one.

## Exact object and frozen objective

All four words use the SAME enclosing M=6 and tau=1/4:

    lambda_s=1+sqrt(2), beta_word=23/24,
    c=(25/24)lambda_s, a=(23/24)/c,
    H0=(), H(j+1)=(Hj,1+a*c^j,Hj), j=0,...,5.

The discovery words are the actual children H3,H4,H5,H6 of that definition.
Their smaller-depth parameters are not recomputed with enclosing3,4,5.
All physical steps and every gradient coordinate, including the terminal
gradient, are retained. The terminal gradient supplies no additional update.

Fact1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the original W=2R normalization and interpolation
convention. Normalize L=1 and x_star=g_star=f_star=0. For independent formal
vectors(x0,g0,...,gn), use x_i=x0-sum_(t<i)h_t*g_t and ALL distinct ordered rows

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.

Both optimum directions and both terminal directions are included. If
Q=F f+A vec(G), the numerical model is

    minimize J=sum w_ij*lambda_ij,
    lambda_ij>=0, F^T lambda=2e_n,
    C=q_target E00+reshape(A^T lambda) PSD,
    q_target=101/[100(1+2sum h)].

Thus the full intended identity is
q_target||x0||^2-2f_n+sum lambda_ij Q_ij=<C,G>, with every value coefficient
cancelled. This level was a feasibility target, not an assumed upper bound.
There is no rank, support, product-kernel, or incoming-terminal restriction.

The exact weights were published BEFORE job1 in plan9be7ee41a1245d03 and
`preregistration.md`. Let N=n+1=2^j. For distinct finite labels i,k, let
ell=bit_length(i XOR k), the depth of their smallest aligned dyadic block.
Use w_ik=2^(ell-1)/N and w_(star,i)=w_(i,star)=1. Terminal n=N-1 uses the
same finite rule in both directions. This ONE linear functional is unchanged
in all cases; there is no second optimization or adaptive regularization.

Conditioning uses K_cond=1+sum h, a scalar distinct from M=6, and
D_cond=diag(1,1/K_cond,...,1/K_cond). The solver constrains
C_scaled=K_cond D_cond C D_cond PSD. Multipliers and value flow are in original
units throughout. Original and scaled maps/matrices and the inverse congruence
are saved. The independent H4 readback checks the inversion explicitly.

## Calibration and feasibility defects

For h=(3/2), the preregistered nonzero reference multipliers are
lambda_(0,star)=lambda_(1,star)=lambda_(1,0)=1. At q=101/400,

 q||x0||^2-2f1+Q_(0,star)+Q_(1,star)+Q_(1,0)
   =||x0/2-g0-g1||^2+||x0||^2/400.

The exact reference objective is5/2. The charged model evaluation had zero
matrix-entry and flow discrepancy against this reference. The calibration
solver returned J=2.499999943615519 with secondary gap1.645353719936793e-8.
Every preregistered calibration check passed. The raw calibration still has
minimum multiplier-1.2612436727442586e-8 and minimum original eigenvalue
-9.956744939364884e-9. Passing numerical tolerances is not exact feasibility.

The following values are copied from the saved in-job summaries. J is the
secondary objective, NOT the original PEP objective2f_n.

| Child | n | q_target | J | Secondary gap | Iterations |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| H3 | 7 | 0.03782489677601333 | 2.853465898662487 | 3.2180014208904595e-9 | 16 |
| H4 | 15 | 0.015182158499335989 | 3.107557434785927 | 1.241881131264222e-8 | 20 |
| H5 | 31 | 0.006138546371301919 | 3.5082763702999706 | 3.408905424606701e-8 | 26 |
| H6 | 63 | 0.0024832359777307244 | 4.175331273969408 | 3.1075637174637905e-8 | 38 |

| Child | Min original multiplier | Min eigenvalue of original C | Max absolute flow defect | Max secondary stationarity defect |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| H3 | -4.467548844850404e-10 | -8.858543968733082e-10 | 2.999822612537173e-13 | 1.1264832400215141e-9 |
| H4 | -7.671545584423017e-10 | -5.321077301619408e-10 | 1.5510535713475214e-13 | 3.198401099524517e-9 |
| H5 | -1.0079188874254564e-9 | -6.393948206750424e-11 | 1.5407675135747922e-12 | 1.1020659940186306e-8 |

There are20,82,362,1381 negative raw multipliers respectively; all are retained.
No eigenvalue or multiplier was clipped. The nonnegativity constraints are in
the model, but none of these floating-point outputs is an exact sign/PSD
certificate. Full spectra and residual arrays are preserved, not only minima.

For the secondary conic dual, let y be the flow equality multiplier, s the
nonnegativity dual, and Z_original=K_cond D_cond Z_scaled D_cond. Its saved
stationarity is w+F y-A vec(Z_original)-s=0; its objective is
-2y_n-q_target Z_original[0,0]. These are the actual primal/dual diagnostics
for J. The nonzero right side w means this dual is not automatically an
original interpolation-feasible transcript or a legal adversarial function.

## Different hierarchy coefficients and the suggested relation

The charged diagnostics compared each parent's two induced children against
the previous SAVED inherited child. The left child includes finite sample
labels0,...,r, and the right child r+1,...,n, where r=(n-1)/2 is the physical
root bridge index. Each comparison also retains both optimum directions.
The scalar fit is a descriptive closed-form least-squares calculation, not
another solver call. The relative residual is normalized by the parent vector.

| Parent / child | Right finite factor | Right finite residual | Right optimum factor | Right optimum residual | Left finite residual |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| H4 / H3 | 1.0007690219603347 | 0.007239491890668989 | 0.6315969265208133 | 0.018536547699762426 | 0.6446437457246462 |
| H5 / H4 | 1.00030299279191 | 0.018844299469799174 | 0.6318115947637603 | 0.021986792708540426 | 0.7859783772146531 |
| H6 / H5 | 1.0002894706516186 | 0.0054076217631775685 | 0.6314755899700785 | 0.024142689287816408 | 0.8786389603659166 |

The right finite and optimum coefficients therefore need separate treatment.
A single scalar copy of a full child does not describe these optimizers.
Even separate scalars do not give exact identities: the displayed residuals
are much larger than numerical stationarity defects. The left finite block
is particularly far from a scalar copy.

The H6 root row illustrates real coefficient differences. All entries below
are original multipliers, with r=31 and the physical h values retained:

| Destination k | Physical level nu_2(k+1) | lambda_(31,k) | lambda_(31,k)/(h_31 h_k) |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| 30 | 0 | 7.416348190399652 | 0.13653774941895674 |
| 29 | 1 | 1.311857903575235 | 0.017032583903911765 |
| 27 | 2 | 0.24672903616397593 | 0.0018396847590614928 |
| 23 | 3 | 0.0476281112112982 | 0.0001715116516773268 |
| 0 | 0 | 1.0386633518121214e-8 | 1.9122181539960158e-10 |

Destinations30 and0 have the same physical level but very different weights.
Position within the hierarchy matters as well as step level. The earlier root
rows show the same concentration near successive dyadic endpoints: at H3,
lambda_(3,2)=0.45711281504070095 and lambda_(3,1)=0.09836338877232588; at H4,
lambda_(7,6)=1.1297980965705121, lambda_(7,5)=0.23467954457630183 and
lambda_(7,3)=0.003942262395092079; at H5, lambda_(15,14)=2.8653798695720165,
lambda_(15,13)=0.5449511942690242 and lambda_(15,11)=0.09483804561591222.
This is descriptive evidence for level- and position-dependent corrections,
not an exact support law. All smaller coefficients remain in the raw data.

Within the top dyadic split of finite samples, the backward cross-block sums
are0.7435105990164873,0.7283108670456813,0.7243282976299297,
0.7252981469443092 for H3 through H6. The forward blocks of H3,H4,H5 have
no entries above1e-7. H6 differs: its forward block has six entries above1e-7,
sum0.0021584484010527287 and maximum0.0015462200468230186. Thus deleting
all forward cross-block coefficients would change the selected H6 result
well beyond its residual scale. This is not an impossibility result for some
other certificate with different support.

The full saved singular spectra also argue against declaring a universal
one-channel correction. H5's backward cross-block leading singular values
are0.22003430918415515 and1.0969626725889478e-7, whereas H6's are
0.1829102833539127 and0.001228526081829379. The H6 forward block has leading
singular values0.0016618026040199432 and3.3281783246296826e-8. These are
threshold-dependent numerical observations; no exact rank is asserted.

The useful next analytical hypothesis is an asymmetric parent construction:
retain most of the right finite block, transport its optimum attachments
separately, and add position-dependent left and cross-block corrections.
The observed near-copy relation, optimum scale, and dyadic endpoint pattern
are clues to derive from the full identity, not premises. The data contradict
a faithful description by one scalar child copy or the previous two-parameter
product kernel; they neither exclude other duals nor prove a recursion.

Incoming terminal sums are1.2896450517851894e-9,7.309053742620733e-9,
3.061772810715e-8,4.16240364021452e-8. These rows were all present in the
model and saved without deletion. Their small numerical values are not exact
zeros. Support counts above1e-7 are28,72,162,416; at1e-9 they are37,153,553,1747.
The threshold sensitivity is another reason to retain the complete arrays.

## Independent original-cone readback

Slot6 imported no solver or solve module. It reconstructed every H4 Q matrix
by explicit outer products, accumulated all original finite value coefficients,
regenerated the SAME enclosing-M6 H4 and weights independently, and undid
the saved congruence. The saved results were:

* All272 ordered rows retained; min multiplier-7.671545584423017e-10.
* Max absolute original flow residual1.5510535713475214e-13.
* Original and scaled matrix differences from the saved solve:0 and0.
* Original minimum eigenvalue-5.321077301619408e-10, unchanged.
* Congruence inverse difference2.7755575615628914e-17.
* Word, target q, exact weight rule, and conditioning-vector differences:0.
* Secondary stationarity maximum3.198401099524517e-9; secondary gap
  1.241881131264222e-8, matching the saved solve.

Source H4 raw SHA256:
`d2ab9e06baa54a2c1398b3ddcde242eef2d47a5554538d4c199c018ceb7d11d0`.
This independently checks model/convention/export agreement. It does not
repair the negative multipliers or eigenvalues and is not formal verification.

## Resource receipt, public evidence, and scope

These times are nested and are not added together. The ledger total is the

Software: Python3.12.14, NumPy2.5.3, SciPy1.18.1, CVXPY1.9.3,

All warnings arrays were empty. No installation or external compute service.

The absolute public directory is
`/opt/danus/runtime/projects/gd-exact-profile-20260926/research/hierarchical_dual_discovery_20261001/`.

Every solve has original/scaled coefficient maps in `model.json`, full original

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000648 -->
# max4 round64: frozen inherited-M secondary-dual discovery

Status before all jobs: numerical discovery only; no theorem, formal call,
original PEP primal maximization, new benchmark computation, or installation.
Guidance970c491889a34251 and TASK.md allocate six whole-child slots of30s,
180 accounted seconds. Reserve the full slot before any mathematical import.
Every import/build/solve/check/pattern calculation/raw export is inside that
external cap. One solver call per solve job. Single threaded existing software.
All prior grants remain closed. Guard1311->1491 is reservation, not elapsed time.

## Exact words and normalization

For every discovery case, the enclosing parameter is M_enclosing=6 and
tau=1/4. Set lambda_s=1+sqrt(2), beta_word=23/24,
c=lambda_s*25/24, a=beta_word/c. Set H0=(),
H(j+1)=(Hj,1+a*c^j,Hj). Solve exactly the specified four children H3,H4,H5,H6.
Their n are7,15,31,63. Never replace their parameters by enclosing j.
Calibration alone uses h=(3/2), n=1. Arithmetic evaluations are binary64.

Use fact1252b8823bb5a290, the original W=2R normalization, L=1,
x_star=g_star=f_star=0, and the full formal basis(x0,g0,...,gn).
Physical x_i=x0-sum_(t<i)h_t*g_t. There is no update using g_n.
For ALL distinct ordered i,j in {star,0,...,n},
Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.
Write Q=F f+A vec(G), with row-major full symmetric Gram entries.
All original rows remain. Diagonal Q_ii=0 identities have no variable.

Fix q_target=101/[100(1+2sum h)] in each case. This is a feasibility target,
not a previously established upper bound. The sole optimization variables
are the original multipliers lambda_ij, all constrained nonnegative.
Require F^T lambda=2e_n and C=q_target E00+reshape(A^T lambda) PSD on
ALL n+2 original coordinates. The identity is
q_target||x0||^2-2f_n+sum lambda_ij Q_ij=<C,G>.
No rank, pair support, terminal direction, product-form, or boundary restriction.

## ONE exact linear objective, unchanged across cases

Let N=n+1=2^j (j=1 for calibration). For distinct finite labels i,k in
{0,...,N-1}, define ell(i,k) as the unique positive integer such that
2^(ell-1)<=i XOR k<2^ell. Thus ell is the depth of their smallest aligned
dyadic block. Set

    w_(i,k)=2^(ell(i,k)-1)/N,
    w_(star,i)=w_(i,star)=1.

Minimize J=sum_(i!=k)w_(i,k)*lambda_(i,k). These positive rational weights
favor fine dyadic pairs. Terminal label n=N-1 uses the SAME finite-label
formula in both directions. The optimum rows always have weight1.
There is no other penalty, tie-break optimization, scan, or adaptive regularizer.
All integer weight numerators and the common denominator N are saved.

## Invertible conditioning and secondary dual diagnostics

Let K_cond=1+sum h, unrelated to M_enclosing. Define
D_cond=diag(1,1/K_cond,...,1/K_cond), C_scaled=K_cond D_cond C D_cond,
A_scaled=A diag(vec(K_cond D_cond outer D_cond)). Constrain C_scaled PSD.
Multipliers, objective, and value flow stay in ORIGINAL units.
No objective rescaling is used. Save C and C_scaled and undo the congruence
explicitly. If Z_scaled is the secondary PSD dual, y its equality dual,
and s its nonnegativity dual, save also Z_original=K_cond D_cond Z_scaled D_cond.
Stationarity is w+F y-A vec(Z_original)-s=0, with y free, s>=0 and Z>=0.
The secondary dual objective is -2*y_n-q_target*Z_original[0,0].
Save this objective, its gap to J, all residuals, and complementarity.
Z_original and y are NOT automatically an original PEP primal transcript:
the secondary stationarity has the nonzero right side w.

## Calibration certificate BEFORE execution

For h=(3/2), set lambda_(0,star)=lambda_(1,star)=lambda_(1,0)=1,
all other original multipliers0. Every sign is nonnegative, finite flow is
(0,2), and at q=101/400 the full identity is

 (101/400)||x0||^2-2f1+Q_(0,star)+Q_(1,star)+Q_(1,0)
   =||x0/2-g0-g1||^2+(1/400)||x0||^2.

The matrix is [[101/400,-1/2,-1/2],[-1/2,1,1],[-1/2,1,1]], PSD as the
displayed sum of squares. The preregistered objective of these multipliers
is5/2 because the two optimum weights are1 and w_(1,0)=1/2.
This is a feasible reference, not the asserted optimum of the secondary LP/SDP.
The charged calibration checks the model reference identity and flow to1e-12,
reference minimum eigenvalue>=-1e-12, then solver status optimal or
optimal_inaccurate, complete outputs, min lambda>=-1e-7, original flow<=1e-7,
min eigenvalue C>=-1e-7, scaled PSD>=-1e-7, J<=5/2+1e-6,
absolute secondary gap<=1e-6, and stationarity<=1e-6.
A failed calibration ends discovery unless the single correction is explicitly
used within the same six-slot allocation. No automatic retry is implemented.

## Frozen jobs and diagnostics

Normal order: calibration,H3,H4,H5,H6,independent_H4_readback.
Exactly one CLARABEL invocation per solve with max_iter120,max_threads1,
time_limit18,tol_gap_abs1e-8,tol_gap_rel1e-8,tol_feas1e-8. External whole-child
30s cap remains controlling. Timeout/error consumes30 accounted seconds.
The sixth slot is solver-free independent reconstruction of saved H4, unless
the sole necessary correction uses it. Failed H4 retires the readback slot.
No seventh job. No post-closure mathematical computation.

Save all original/scaled coefficient maps, positions, pair labels, weights,
original multipliers, full matrices and spectra, sign/flow/PSD residuals,
secondary primal/dual/KKT diagnostics, settings, actual precision, iterations,
counts, and nested times. Do not clip eigenvalues, threshold stored raw arrays,
or discard small multipliers. Structural thresholds1e-7 and1e-9 are descriptive.

Within each charged solve, summarize every aligned dyadic block's two directed
cross-half multiplier matrices: sums, extrema, thresholded support, full singular
values. Preserve all bridge rows and terminal/optimum rows. Report queried-prefix
lambda_ik/(h_i h_k) by direction and root destination level, with full root lists.
At H4,H5,H6 compare each induced child including optimum attachments to the
previous SAVED inherited child: best scalar least-squares factor and residual,
plus finite-only and optimum-only comparisons. These are diagnostics of the
chosen numerical optimizer, not support restrictions or additional optimizations.
Save both root directed cross-block singular spectra and all cone eigenvalues.

The independent H4 program imports no solver or solve module and uses explicit
outer products and flow accumulation from the saved original ordered labels.
It regenerates H4 under M_enclosing6, reconstructs q, all coefficients, signs,
flow, original PSD and conditioning inversion, and checks secondary stationarity
using direct quadratic forms. Preserve every residual; agreement is not a proof.

## Scope and provenance

ad24079f80cd6a3a is old standalone-m primal discovery; no old call is reused.
dd4045b39aa0c257 is an unverified scoped product-kernel obstruction, not an
impossibility premise. The public old solve_case.py was read for its original
Q coefficient convention; its runner is never invoked and its ledger unchanged.
Only the shared existing Python launcher supplies numerical packages.
The accepted fact supplies the model. Matlas returned two inapplicable lemmas;
web found the known silver-schedule paper2309.16530, whose different schedule
is not imported as a theorem about these inherited words. No new literature
premise, external_refs entry, benchmark comparison, or formal claim is made.

All new artifacts are under the assigned absolute public directory, except
updates to the specifically assigned canonical ledger and append-only existing
worker local memory. Hash the frozen sources and this document before job1.
Close and retire unused credit at round end. No automatic renewal.

# max2 round59 handoff

Status: one allocated analytical round complete; all new mathematics
UNVERIFIED. Original exact-profile Goal OPEN. No formal gate or further
round was authorized by this task.

Assignment: finite realizable perturbation of the exact same-word Huber
transcript on tau1/4. Completed baseline58, assigned completed ceiling59,
attempted ceiling60. Originating and latest-read guidance7b221a98307db859.

Complete joint evidence: proof_attempt6d0784199ba9ce6f, public file
`elapsed_clock_perturbation.md`, SHA256
`58bfdea59c2faec59068a5d62f38382bdecad2e37e546e1b06353b1679d482ea`.
Plan80f6f693d6dac1ed; counterexamplebd6c4bd06ca32f1a;
scoped dead_end38ea9b001fdf332e. No fact_id was created.

## What was actually constructed

For a positive physical word with S=sum h>=2, use the complete original
clocks P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t and T_i=sum_(t<i)h_t P_t. In R^2 set x0=e0 and

    g_i=sigma[(1-2epsilon P_i/S)e0+sqrt(epsilon)(P_i/S)e1],
    x_i=e0-P_i sigma e0-T_i(sigma/S)(-2epsilon e0+sqrt(epsilon)e1).

All0<epsilon<=1/16 are allowed. Put eta=T_n/S, alpha=1-2epsilon,
M=1+2S, J=1+2eta+4alpha(S-eta), D=M alpha^2+epsilon J,
sigma=alpha/D. One shared value array has terminal
f_n=<g_n,x_n>-||g_n||^2/2 and exact trapezoidal decrements.

The full Gram is explicit and PSD. The projected scalar derivative has
slope1/(d-P_i), d=2S/(1+4epsilon)>S+1; this pays every finite directed
interpolation row. Outgoing optimum heights decrease to zero at n;
all opposite optimum rows retain a uniform strict margin. Only after
these checks, accepted1252b8823bb5a290 constructs one static interpolant.
The strict opposite-optimum rows imply that this function has unique
minimum0 and true initial radius1. Its smoothness1 is sharp.

## Quantitative outcome and precise failure

The ORIGINAL terminal optimum row is an exact upper bound over every
common scalar scale and every reselected shared value array. The displayed
legal construction attains it:

    V_epsilon=alpha^2/(M alpha^2+epsilon J)<H_base=1/M.

At the fixed amplitude epsilon=1/16,

    (7/8)H_base<=V_epsilon<=(49/54)H_base.

The perturbation therefore remains at the benchmark scale but loses a
fixed fraction. Accepted5fd396eba75651e7 supplies the independent same-word
comparison: for all m>=1536 on the unchanged tau1/4 hierarchy,

    B_(2^m-1)V_epsilon<(49/54)(237/250)<1.

The failure survives arbitrary scalar retuning in this specified class.
If g_i=sigma[(1-delta P_i/S)e0+sqrt(nu)(P_i/S)e1], with0<delta<1,nu>0,
then, writing A=1-delta, the terminal row gives

    2f_n<=A^2/[M A^2+2delta A(S-eta)+nu(1+2eta)]<1/M.

This is a necessary bound, not an assertion that every retuning is legal.
Omitting the scalar correction altogether fails the first original
pair: Q01+Q10=nu sigma^2 h0^2/S^2>0, independently of all values.

## Remaining interface and resource handoff

No general Huber optimality, no unrestricted W upper bound, no finite
Huber-beating example, and no original all-word lower theorem was obtained.
The limitation is affine dependence on elapsed mass, initial-gradient
alignment with the unit radius, and a positive terminal longitudinal
component. Nonlinear temporal kernels, different initial correlations,
and terminal reversals are outside the obstruction. This is a useful
finite legality/payment calibration for a separately assigned future
kernel, not authorization for another mechanism or round.

All mathematical programs, numerical evaluations, arithmetic replays,
executed recurrences, solvers, benchmark DP, verifier/fact_submit calls,
installations, consults, and subagents: ZERO. Mechanism repairs: ZERO.
No old computation grant was reopened. Only administrative reads,
literature retrieval, symbolic reasoning and text/integrity persistence
were performed. The original task and latest guidance still agree.
The post-return outer-loop counter has not been observed.

The proof was read back in full. Broad initial recall output was truncated;
all required failure evidence and exact relevant scopes were recovered in
bounded reads before the policy was selected. The exact local primary
paper1502.05666 was read for terminology, and no external theorem was
imported. New correctness authority remains absent because formal
verification was explicitly outside this round's allocation.

# A finite Huber perturbation with paid interpolation slack

Status: COMPLETE JOINT ANALYTICAL DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. Worker max2, round59,
under TASK.md and master_guidance7b221a98307db859. Plan80f6f693d6dac1ed.
No new fact, verifier call, numerical evaluation, mathematical program,
executed recurrence, solver, benchmark replay, consult, installation or
subagent. This is one assigned round and does not renew itself.

## 1. Result and scope

The selected perturbation is realizable at a fixed, nonzero amplitude.
It retains every physical step, every queried sample, the unqueried
terminal, both directions of every interpolation pair, and initial norm1.
Its transverse gradient is born at zero and grows with elapsed physical
step mass; its covariance is correlated. A decreasing scalar mean pays
for that growth. The payment gives an exact finite construction, rather
than a first-order direction with an uncontrolled remainder.

This mechanism does not improve Huber. For every positive finite word
with mass S>=2, its exact optimized payoff at amplitude epsilon=1/16 is
between (7/8)H_base and (49/54)H_base, where H_base=1/(1+2S).
The constructed static function has a UNIQUE minimizer at0, so this is
also its true closest-distance payoff. A necessary terminal row proves
the upper bound even if all values and the common gradient scale are
reselected. A broader terminal-row calculation excludes improvement by
any positive scalar retuning within the stated affine-clock class while
the terminal gradient still has positive initial-direction component.

On the unchanged tau1/4 hierarchy this is a genuine benchmark-order
perturbation, but with a fixed loss. It supplies neither a finite
counterexample to Huber optimality nor a lower witness above1/B_n.
It excludes no nonlinear temporal covariance policy, arbitrary initial
correlation, terminal sign reversal, or unrestricted smooth-convex
function. The original all-word exact-profile problem remains OPEN.

Only accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 is used for the interpolation convention
and, AFTER all rows have been proved, its explicit conjugate interpolant.
Only accepted fact5fd396eba75651e7 is used for its separately stated
same-word Huber/benchmark comparison. Every new calculation is proved
jointly here; earlier unverified findings are not premises.

## 2. Original word, normalization, and notation

For the assigned hierarchy fix an integer m>=2 and put

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), beta_word=1-1/(4m),
    c=lambda*(1+1/(4m)), a_word=beta_word/c,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a_word*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m.

The physical word is h=H_m, with true length n=2^m-1. Every child keeps
these SAME enclosing-m parameters. There is no terminal-step deletion or
word compression. Since the first H_2 has three steps greater than1,
its mass, and hence the mass of H_m, exceeds3.

The construction below in fact works for ANY positive finite word
h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)) whose mass S=sum_(t<n)h_t is at least2. Define

    P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t,
    T_i=sum_(t<i)h_t*P_t,       0<=i<=n,
    T=T_n, eta=T/S, M=1+2S, H_base=1/M.                 (1)

Thus P_0=T_0=0 and P_n=S. These are written finite sums, not executed
recurrences. Expanding the square of the step sum gives

    2T=S^2-sum_(t<n)h_t^2,
    0<=eta<=S/2.                                       (2)

Use Euclidean space R^2 with orthonormal vectors e0,e1. Set
x_star=g_star=0 and f_star=0; x0=e0 has norm exactly1. Smoothness is1.
For sampled vectors and values the original residual convention is

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.         (3)

All ordered distinct pairs i,j in {star,0,...,n} must satisfy Q_ij<=0.
At the end, a general advertised smoothness L>0 is obtained by using
L*F and physical steps h_i/L. The normalized error stays2f_n.

## 3. One explicit finite policy

Choose an amplitude epsilon with

    0<epsilon<=1/16, alpha=1-2epsilon,
    J=1+2eta+4alpha*(S-eta),
    D=M*alpha^2+epsilon*J,
    sigma=alpha/D.                                    (4)

In particular 7/8<=alpha<1, J>0, D>0, and sigma>0. Define EVERY original
gradient, including the terminal gradient, by

    g_i=sigma*((1-2epsilon*P_i/S)*e0
                         +sqrt(epsilon)*(P_i/S)*e1).   (5)

Let

    v=sigma*e0,
    w=(sigma/S)*(-2epsilon*e0+sqrt(epsilon)*e1).

Then g_i=v+P_i*w. Define every position by the actual GD substitution:

    x_i=e0-P_i*v-T_i*w.                                (6)

Equations(1),(5),(6) give x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g_i exactly.
No averaging of different functions or transcripts occurs.

The full Gram matrix of (x0,g_0,...,g_n) is the Gram of these displayed
two-dimensional vectors and is therefore positive semidefinite. More
explicitly, write q_i=P_i/S. Its entries are

    <x0,x0>=1,
    <x0,g_i>=sigma*(1-2epsilon*q_i),
    <g_i,g_j>=sigma^2*((1-2epsilon*q_i)*(1-2epsilon*q_j)
                                             +epsilon*q_i*q_j). (7)

The transverse covariance is epsilon*sigma^2*q_i*q_j. It is nonzero,
positive semidefinite, and zero at the initial query. It has strong
cross-time correlations. Indeed all pairwise gradient inner products
are positive, since their e0 components are at least7sigma/8 and their
e1 components are nonnegative. This is outside the expected-orthogonality
hypothesis of accepted0e7e78056dc6258c, without using that fact as a bound.

Define one common value array by

    f_n=<g_n,x_n>-||g_n||^2/2,
    f_i=f_n+sum_(t=i)^(n-1)
                    (h_t/2)*<g_t+g_(t+1),g_t>, i<n.   (8)

Thus the exact value differences are

    f_(i+1)-f_i=-(h_i/2)*<g_i+g_(i+1),g_i>.            (9)

Every value in(8) is attached to an original sample. No value is discarded
or assigned separately for different inequality checks.

## 4. The exact terminal value and the initial scale

Equations(5),(6) give

    g_n=sigma*(alpha*e0+sqrt(epsilon)*e1),
    x_n=(1-sigma*S+2epsilon*sigma*eta)*e0
                                      -sqrt(epsilon)*sigma*eta*e1.

Expanding the terminal expression in(8) gives

    2f_n=2sigma*alpha-sigma^2*D,                        (10)

because

    D=2alpha*S+alpha^2+epsilon
                         +2epsilon*eta*(1-2alpha)
     =M*alpha^2+epsilon*[1+2eta+4alpha*(S-eta)].        (11)

The choice sigma=alpha/D therefore yields

    2f_n=alpha^2/D=sigma^2*D.                          (12)

We will need two elementary bounds, both finite and uniform:

    D>=M*alpha^2>1,
    1/sigma=D/alpha>=M*alpha>S+1.                      (13)

For the first, M>=5 and alpha>=7/8. For the second,
(7/8)(2S+1)-(S+1)=(6S-1)/8>0 when S>=2.

The factor sigma is explicitly optimized, not kept at its unperturbed
value. Hence the loss proved later cannot be repaired merely by retuning
the common scalar gradient level.

## 5. Payment for EVERY finite directed interpolation row

Put z=||w||^2>0 and

    d=-<v,w>/z=2S/(1+4epsilon),
    u=w/sqrt(z), r_i=<u,x_i>, k_i=<u,g_i>.             (14)

Here u is a unit vector; d is an auxiliary scalar, not a physical step.
Since epsilon<=1/16 and S>=2,

    d>=8S/5, d-S>=3S/5>=6/5>1.                       (15)

By(6), the projected position increments and gradient increments are

    r_(i+1)-r_i=sqrt(z)*h_i*(d-P_i)>0,
    k_(i+1)-k_i=sqrt(z)*h_i>0.                        (16)

Define k(r) on[r_0,r_n] to be the continuous function affine on each
interval[r_i,r_(i+1)] and equal to k_i at r_i. Its slope on that
interval is exactly

    1/(d-P_i) <= ell:=1/(d-S)<1.                       (17)

Thus k is nondecreasing and ell-Lipschitz. This is the new available
payment. The longitudinal decrease in(5) supplies a finite scalar
curvature margin while the transverse component grows. No signed
quadratic cost is dropped or left to an unknown suffix budget.

The perpendicular component b=v-<u,v>*u of g_i is independent of i.
On a single interval, affine integration of k and(16) gives

    <b,x_(i+1)-x_i> + integral_(r_i)^(r_(i+1)) k(r)dr
       =<(g_i+g_(i+1))/2,x_(i+1)-x_i>
       =-(h_i/2)*<g_i+g_(i+1),g_i>.

Summing these EXACT equalities and using(9), for all i<j,

    f_j-f_i=<b,x_j-x_i>+integral_(r_i)^(r_j) k(r)dr.    (18)

Let Delta=k_j-k_i=sqrt(z)*(P_j-P_i)>0. Since k has Lipschitz
constant at most ell, r_j-r_i>=Delta/ell. On the final subinterval
of length Delta/ell one has
k(r)-k_i >= Delta-ell*(r_j-r); elsewhere k(r)-k_i>=0.
Consequently

    integral_(r_i)^(r_j)(k(r)-k_i)dr>=Delta^2/(2ell).

On the initial subinterval of the same length,
k_j-k(r)>=Delta-ell*(r-r_i), so also

    integral_(r_i)^(r_j)(k_j-k(r))dr>=Delta^2/(2ell).

The difference g_j-g_i is parallel to u and has norm Delta. Applying
(18) to these two area inequalities proves BOTH original residuals:

    Q_ij <= -(d-S-1)*z*(P_j-P_i)^2/2 <0,
    Q_ji <= -(d-S-1)*z*(P_j-P_i)^2/2 <0.              (19)

This proof covers every pair, however remote, and includes j=n. It is
not an adjacent-row test or a first-order Taylor expansion. Every term
of the finite-amplitude policy has been retained.

## 6. Payment for both optimum directions

For each finite i set

    E_i=<g_i,x_i>-||g_i||^2/2-f_i.

By(8), E_n=0 and Q_(i,star)=-E_i. To check all earlier E_i, write
P=P_i, Tloc=T_i, h=h_i. From(6), (9), and g_(i+1)=g_i+h*w,
direct expansion gives

    E_(i+1)-E_i
      =h*[<w,x_i>-(1+h/2)*<w,g_i>-(h/2)*z]
      =h*z*[d*(P+1+h/2-1/sigma)
                           -Tloc-P-(h/2)*(P+1)].       (20)

For the second line use
<w,e0>/z=-d/sigma, <w,v>/z=-d, and g_i=v+P*w.
Since P+h<=S and 1/sigma>S+1 by(13), the bracket in(20) is strictly
negative. Thus E_i>E_(i+1), and E_n=0 implies

    Q_(i,star)<0 for every i<n, Q_(n,star)=0.          (21)

For the OTHER optimum direction,

    Q_(star,i)=-f_i+||g_i||^2/2.

All gradient coordinates in(5) are nonnegative, with a strictly positive
e0 component. Equation(9) therefore shows f_i>=f_n. The gradient norm
decreases with P: the derivative of ||v+P*w||^2 as a written polynomial
in P is 2z*(P-d)<0 for0<=P<=S. Hence ||g_i||^2<=||g_0||^2=sigma^2.
Using(12),(13) gives the uniform strict margin

    f_i-||g_i||^2/2 >= (sigma^2/2)*(D-1)>0,
    Q_(star,i) <= -(sigma^2/2)*(D-1)<0.                (22)

This includes i=n. Equations(19),(21),(22) are every original ordered
interpolation inequality. The initially slack optimum rows have not
been omitted. The perturbation preserves their strict signs at every
allowed finite epsilon.

## 7. One static function, and the TRUE radius

Only now invoke the sufficiency construction proved in accepted
fact1252b8823bb5a290. For y in R^2 define

    K_star(y)=||y||^2/2,
    K_i(y)=<x_i,y>-f_i+||y-g_i||^2/2, 0<=i<=n,
    K(y)=max{K_star(y),K_0(y),...,K_n(y)},
    F(x)=sup_(y in R^2)(<x,y>-K(y)).                  (23)

The full Gram(7) is PSD and every row has already been proved.
The cited accepted construction therefore gives one everywhere finite
convex differentiable F with1-Lipschitz gradient, interpolating ALL the
triples(x_i,g_i,f_i), including the terminal and the optimum triple.
It has F(0)=0=min F. The physical GD trajectory of this ONE function
is exactly(6) by induction from its prescribed queried gradients.

The minimizer is actually unique. Equation(22) gives

    K_i(0)=-f_i+||g_i||^2/2<0=K_star(0)

for every finite i. There are finitely many i, so continuity gives a
neighborhood of0 on which K=K_star. Therefore the subdifferential of
K at0 is the singleton{0}. In(23) the unique maximizing y is grad F(x);
grad F(x)=0 holds exactly when x belongs to that subdifferential.
Thus argmin F={0}, and

    dist(x0,argmin F)=||e0||=1.                       (24)

This proves the closest-radius assertion instead of substituting a
convenient optimum for an unknown closer one. It also shows the advertised
smoothness1 is sharp: locally K(y)=||y||^2/2, hence F(x)=||x||^2/2 near0.
For this last claim, at sufficiently small y the differentiable K has
gradient y; strong convexity makes that y the unique maximizer for x=y.

## 8. Exact family optimum and the loss at finite amplitude

Keep S,T,epsilon and the vectors(5),(6), but allow ANY positive scalar
scale sigma and ANY common values from a legal transcript with the same
optimum sample0. The necessary original terminal row alone gives

    2f_n<=2sigma*alpha-D*sigma^2
         =alpha^2/D-D*(sigma-alpha/D)^2.              (25)

Here D depends on S,T,epsilon, not on sigma. The complete legal
construction(4)--(24) attains equality at sigma=alpha/D with true radius1.
Therefore, in the stated class with0 as its radius1 minimizer, the exact
maximum over sigma and all values is

    V_epsilon(h)=alpha^2/(M*alpha^2+epsilon*J),
    V_epsilon/H_base
          =alpha^2/(alpha^2+epsilon*J/M)<1.            (26)

This class definition retains x0=e0 and the initial gradient g0=sigma e0.
It does not secretly optimize another minimum point or a rotated initial
gradient. The attaining member already has the true closest minimum0.

At the fixed nonzero amplitude epsilon=1/16, alpha=7/8. From eta in[0,S/2]
and 2-4alpha<0,

    1+(2alpha+1)*S <= J <= 1+4alpha*S.

The right bound implies J/M<=2alpha because 1<=2alpha. For S>=1,
the left bound implies J/M>=(2alpha+2)/3; after cross multiplication
this assertion is equivalent to (2alpha-1)*(S-1)>=0. Thus

    alpha^2/(alpha^2+2epsilon*alpha)
       <=V_epsilon/H_base
       <=alpha^2/(alpha^2+epsilon*(2alpha+2)/3).

Substituting the displayed rational alpha and epsilon by symbolic
simplification gives

    7/8 <= V_(1/16)(h)/H_base(h) <=49/54<1.           (27)

These bounds concern an actual nonzero finite perturbation and hold
uniformly in the word length; no amplitude tends to zero with m.
The exact gain over Huber is NEGATIVE:

    V_epsilon-H_base
       =-epsilon*J/[M*(M*alpha^2+epsilon*J)].          (28)

In particular at epsilon=1/16 the relative loss is at least5/54.
The full expression, including every higher power of epsilon contained
in alpha, sigma and D, is retained in(26),(28). There is no remainder
to estimate or extrapolation from a tangent cone.

At epsilon=0 the limiting vectors have sigma=1/M, g_i=e0/M and
x_i=(1-P_i/M)e0. Equation(8) becomes

    f_i=(1/M)*(1-P_i/M)-1/(2M^2).

This is exactly the same-word Huber transcript. All finite rows and
all finite-to-optimum rows are then tight. The policies(4)--(8) therefore
give a genuine finite deformation of that transcript.

## 9. Retuning the scalar correction does not rescue this class

There is a broader necessary obstruction which does NOT assume(8).
Keep x0=e0, the same physical word and clocks, but choose any

    sigma>0, 0<delta<1, nu>0,
    g_i=sigma*((1-delta*P_i/S)*e0+sqrt(nu)*(P_i/S)*e1),
    x_i=e0-sum_(t<i)h_t*g_t.                          (29)

Allow all function values to be chosen freely and require the original
optimum triple0. Set A=1-delta>0. Direct expansion of Q_(n,star)<=0 gives

    2f_n <= 2sigma*A-D_gen*sigma^2,
    D_gen=M*A^2+2delta*A*(S-eta)+nu*(1+2eta).          (30)

Both extra terms in D_gen are strictly positive. Therefore every legal
radius1 transcript in(29), with arbitrary values, satisfies

    2f_n <= A^2/D_gen <1/M.                           (31)

The equality-producing scale would be A/D_gen; its mere existence as a
number is not a claim that every delta,nu is legal. Legality and equality
were established only for the finite subfamily delta=2epsilon, nu=epsilon
in Sections3--7. The necessary bound(31) nevertheless rules out improvement
through arbitrary positive retuning of that scalar drift, covariance size,
common scale, or value array inside(29).

To see exactly where the correction matters, omit it: delta=0,nu>0.
At the first physical step, g0=sigma e0 and
g1-g0=sigma*sqrt(nu)*(h0/S)e1, whereas x1-x0=-h0*sigma e0.
For ANY two values, summing the two original rows yields

    Q_01+Q_10=||g1-g0||^2-<g1-g0,x1-x0>
             =nu*sigma^2*h0^2/S^2>0.                 (32)

Thus the zero-initial transverse covariance is not itself a legal
addition to Huber. The mean decrease really pays an otherwise impossible
row. For delta<0, the extra positive longitudinal contribution already
makes the same pair fail. The upper scope delta<1 in(29) is a substantive
restriction: a terminal sign reversal is NOT excluded by this argument.

This is a normalization/objective obstruction in an explicit class,
not a global impossibility claim. It comes from the ORIGINAL terminal
row with the full radius fixed, not from an unproved matrix sign,
orthogonality assumption, or unavailable boundary covariance budget.

## 10. Scale on the unchanged tau1/4 hierarchy

For m>=1536, accepted fact5fd396eba75651e7, Section10 and its statement,
gives for this SAME H_m

    B_(2^m-1)*H_base(H_m)<237/250,
    lim_(m->infinity) B_(2^m-1)*H_base(H_m)
                              =Z_1/(2exp(1/4))>0.    (33)

Here B_n is the original all-split Zhang--Jiang denominator, and
Z_1=lim lambda^(-m)B_(2^m-1)>0 is its accepted unconditional ray limit.
No seed assumption, replacement benchmark, or executed benchmark
recurrence is involved. The predecessor is used ONLY for(33), not as
an upper bound on unrestricted W_n.

Combining(27),(33) proves, at every m>=1536,

    B_(2^m-1)*V_(1/16)(H_m)
                     <(49/54)*(237/250)<1.            (34)

Also

    liminf B_(2^m-1)*V_(1/16)(H_m)
                      >=(7/8)*Z_1/(2exp(1/4))>0.     (35)

Hence the prospective signal was not trivially diluted below the
benchmark scale: a fixed transverse amplitude survives at that scale.
Its exact payment has the wrong sign for improvement, by(28),(30).
Equations(34),(35) are sufficient quantitative scale information; no
new infinite-product or moment asymptotic is needed.

The original target is still whether W_n^*/U_n^ZJ tends to1, with
W_n^*=inf_(h>=0)W_n(h) and U_n^ZJ=1/B_n. The affirmative direction
needs a uniform all-word lower bound. The present family does not give
even an improved lower bound on its assigned hierarchy, much less that
separate uniform comparison.

## 11. Prior failures, literature, and what is actually new

Required provenance was recalled before choosing the policy:

- 19f0eaeb0e7c3518 retains full conditional covariance but cannot pay the

JOINT UNVERIFIED, max3 round64. This is a delimited part of the complete single proof attempt 6506975d9ac35dd0; no circular result is an accepted premise. ## 1. Frozen definitions and jointly checked local identities

All spaces and inner products are real Euclidean. Put

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), k=floor(sqrt(m)), J=m-k, beta=1/k,
    eta_word=1-1/(4m), c=lambda*(1+1/(4m)), a_word=eta_word/c,
    d=c-2, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,b_j,H_j),
    b_j=1+a_word*c^j, S_j=sum H_j, n=2^m-1.

Every child retains this SAME enclosing m. Checkpoint i is the first
occurrence of level l_i=J+i-1. Its post-step query is t_i=2^l_i.
The ordinary blocks have masses s_1=S_J and s_i=S_(J+i-2) for i>=2.
The final suffix starts at t_k=2^(m-1), has word H_(m-1), length
N=2^(m-1)-1 and mass S=S_(m-1). Set S_pre=S_(m-1)+b_(m-1).

In suffix entry, exit and persistence formulas, time0 means the actual
global query t_k, and timeN means the actual global terminal query n:
x(t)=x_(t_k+t). The checkpoint labels t_i and the full-prefix integral
endpoints T use global physical time. No initial state is replaced when
passing between these two indexing conventions.

For0<=q<=1 set

    p(q)=beta+sqrt((1+beta)^2-(q+beta)^2),
    e=sqrt(1+2beta), C=beta+e, kappa=1+beta^2,
    K=conv({0} union {(p(q),-q):0<=q<=1}).

The entire body's sections are beta q<=p<=p(q). Its horizontal cap is
C and its vertical cap is1. For compact convex B containing0 put
E_B(w)=max_(g in B)(<g,w>-||g||^2/2). Its gradient is Pi_B(w).
Hub_L denotes the one-sided Huber with gradient clip(r,0,L).

The local backward coordinates u_i,y_i use the ACTUAL order of the
ordinary block and the full circle, as explicitly defined and derived
in Appendix A. Put M_i=u_i+y_i/beta. Before running GD fix

    ell_1=0, D_1=1, T_i=b_(l_i)/2-1,
    sigma_i=D_i/M_i, D_(i+1)=sigma_i T_i,
    ell_(i+1)=sigma_i y_i, A_i=ell_i+ell_(i+1)/beta,
    Phi_i(X,Y)=E_(sigma_i K)(X-A_i,Y-ell_(i+1)),
    delta=D_(k+1)/(1+S),
    F(x)=1/2 sum_(i=1)^k Phi_i(x_i,x_(i+1))
                     +1/2 Hub_delta(x_(k+1)-ell_(k+1)), x_0=e_1.

F is convex1-smooth, nonnegative, and minimized at0. Its COMPLETE
minimum set and TRUE closest initial radius are

    C_min={x: x_i<=A_i,
               x_(i+1)>=beta*(x_i-ell_i), 1<=i<=k;
                                    x_(k+1)<=ell_(k+1)},
    R_m^2=dist(e_1,C_min)^2=(u_1^2+y_1^2)/M_1^2.

The proof in Appendix A retains every minimum row, physical query,
terminal value and gradient. All ordered interpolation inequalities and
the full Euclidean Gram condition hold because this is one actual
globally defined smooth convex function. No middle coordinate is renamed1.

At EVERY actual physical query, including before the final suffix, write

    (P_i,-Q_i)=Pi_(sigma_i K)(x_i-A_i,x_(i+1)-ell_(i+1)),
    U_i=P_i-beta Q_i, L_i=C sigma_i,
    P_(k+1)=clip(x_(k+1)-ell_(k+1),0,delta), Q_0=0.

The true update is x_j^+=x_j-alpha(P_j-Q_(j-1)), alpha=h/2.
Before t_k the full Q_k is retained. During the final suffix Q_k=0
because the isolated last scalar stays above its threshold. In that
suffix U_k=P_k and its retired gradient is P_k-Q_(k-1).

For a middle component define r_i=x_i-A_i,
v_i=ell_(i+1)-x_(i+1), z_i=beta r_i+v_i and W_i=r_i+e(v_i)_+.
The full residual equations, directly from the coordinate update, are

    r_i^+=r_i+alpha(Q_(i-1)-P_i),
    v_i^+=v_i+alpha(P_(i+1)-Q_i),
    z_i^+=z_i-alpha[kappa Q_i-beta Q_(i-1)-beta Q_(i+1)
                                      +beta U_i-U_(i+1)].       (1)

They are not independent pair evolutions. In cone coordinates the exact
envelope is

    Phi_i=max {U r_i+Q z_i-[(U+beta Q)^2+Q^2]/2:
           0<=Q<=sigma_i,
           0<=U<=sigma_i p(Q/sigma_i)-beta Q}.                  (2)

Thus r_i<=0 implies U_i=0, Q_i=clip(z_i/kappa,0,sigma_i).
If x_j<=ell_j, (2) forces U_j=0. If x_(j+1)>=ell_(j+1),
maximizing over Q forces Q_j=0. These are state-dependent deductions.

At its OWN checkpoint the actual state is

    r_i(t_i)=-beta D_(i+1), v_i(t_i)=-D_(i+1),
    z_i(t_i)=-kappa D_(i+1).                                  (3)

For T>=t_i define the finite ACTUAL full-word integrals

    I_j^P(i,T)=sum_(u=t_i)^(T-1)(h_u/2) P_j(u),
    I_j^Q(i,T)=sum_(u=t_i)^(T-1)(h_u/2) Q_j(u).

The complete remaining word is H_(l_i),b_(l_i+1),H_(l_i+1),...,
b_(m-1),H_(m-1). No source in its prefix is deleted. Summing (1) gives

    r_i(T)=-beta D_(i+1)-I_i^P+I_(i-1)^Q,
    v_i(T)=-D_(i+1)+I_(i+1)^P-I_i^Q,
    z_i(T)=-kappa D_(i+1)-beta I_i^P+beta I_(i-1)^Q
                                      +I_(i+1)^P-I_i^Q.        (4)

In particular these are the ACTUAL initialized suffix residuals at
T=t_k. The previous coordinate determining Q_(i-1)(t_i) has the entire
physical history; its own-checkpoint margin is not substituted.

The actual projection gives the unconditional terminal bound

    Phi_i(x_n)<=L_i(r_i(n))_++sigma_i(z_i(n))_+.                (5)

The joint derivation in Appendix A gives uniform scale relations
sigma_(i+1)/sigma_i->lambda-2, R_m^2->1,
D_(k+1)->(lambda-2)exp(-2) and

    (B_n/R_m^2) sigma_1^2(1+K_k+2^m)->0,
    K_k=20k^2 exp(2k), sum_i sigma_i^2<=2sigma_1^2              (6)

eventually. B_n is the ORIGINAL all-split benchmark of fact04219554e41d9750.
Its unconditional accepted ray limit is Z_1=lim lambda^(-m)B_(2^m-1)>0.
Appendix A jointly derives both endpoint terminal losses tending to0
and the isolated tail contribution T_m->T_infty=Z_1 exp(-17/4)/(2lambda)
with T_infty<5/64. Those circle/tail assertions remain unverified here.


### B3. An exact scoped failure of a source-free horizontal-exit reset

The claim that every horizontal exit automatically has z_i=O(sigma_i)
is false for the SAME frozen function, even with no incoming gradient
and no downstream horizontal gradient. The following is an ALTERNATIVE
state test, not an initialized trajectory or a lower witness.

Choose2<=i<=k-2 and a physical available h=b_(m-2), alpha=h/2, with
alpha>1 and alpha beta>C/2. Choose q in(0,1) close to1 and write p=p(q).
Set

    t=[(alpha-1)sigma_i p+L_i/2]/(p-beta)>0,
    r_i= sigma_i p+t(p-beta)=alpha sigma_i p+L_i/2,
    v_i= sigma_i q+t(q+beta),
    x_i=A_i+r_i, x_(i+1)=ell_(i+1)-v_i,
    x_(i+2)=ell_(i+2).

The vector (r_i,-v_i)-(sigma_i p,-sigma_i q)
is t(p-beta,-q-beta), an outward circle normal. Thus its ACTUAL
projection is(P_i,-Q_i)=(sigma_i p,-sigma_i q).

All earlier components can be made zero at both samples by the explicit
recursive choice x_j=min(A_j,ell_j+x_(j+1)/beta)-sigma_j for j=i-1,...,1,
using the smaller of the initial and proposed next boundary coordinate
at j=i-1; also enforce x_1<=A_1-beta D_2. Earlier coordinates then have
zero gradients and do not move. All later coordinates after i+2 are0,
except the isolated scalar x_(k+1)=ell_(k+1)+D_(k+1).
The next component has input below ell_(i+1) and output ell_(i+2),
so P_(i+1)=Q_(i+1)=0; all other retired components also vanish.

The actual step therefore has

    r_i^+=L_i/2,
    v_i^+=v_i-alpha sigma_i q
        =(alpha-1)sigma_i beta(p+q)/(p-beta)
                                  +(L_i/2)(q+beta)/(p-beta)>0,
    z_i^+=beta L_i/2+v_i^+.                                 (23)

As q approaches1, p-beta approaches0 while beta>0. For any proposed
constant cap-scale reset, (23) exceeds it. The new input is below L_i,
while the new actual edge residual is arbitrarily large. The next
component remains zero since its input is still below its translation;
all earlier zero-cone choices remain valid since x_i^+>A_i>=ell_i.
The isolated tail excess stays positive for this legal suffix step.

Thus terminal/exit input information alone does not supply a bounded
edge state. The tested normal parameter and huge initial downstream
deficit are not asserted reachable from e_1. The actual initialized
exit debt(21),(22) remains OPEN, not refuted. This is a new exit-reset
failure, distinct from the previously screened cross-time and
simultaneous-checkpoint-margin claims.


### C2. Pay the source inside the signed terminal-gradient identity

Fix the ACTUAL terminal local projection p_N=P_i(n), q_N=Q_i(n).
The full own-checkpoint identity from the maximizing formula is

    Phi_i(x_n)=-beta D_(i+1)p_N-D_(i+1)q_N
         -(p_N^2+q_N^2)/2
         -sum_(u=t_i)^(n-1)(h_u/2)
              [p_N(P_i-Q_(i-1))-q_N(P_(i+1)-Q_i)](u).       (28)

This is algebra for the ACTUAL terminal support, not a generic
cross-time sign claim. Its full Q_k before the final root is retained.
Split exactly at t_(i+1). By Q_i=0 and(24), the large initialized
-D_(i+1)q_N is paid by the completely known successor P integral.
The resulting equality is

    Phi_i(x_n)=-beta D_(i+1)p_N
              +[beta D_(i+2)-ell_(i+2)/beta]q_N
              +p_N J_i^in-(p_N^2+q_N^2)/2
         -sum_(u=t_(i+1))^(n-1)(h_u/2)
              [p_N(P_i-Q_(i-1))-q_N(P_(i+1)-Q_i)](u).       (29)

All old/future gradients and all later dissipation remain. The
remaining word starts H_(l_i+1),b_(l_i+2),H_(l_i+2),...,
b_(m-1),H_(m-1); for i=k-1 it is exactly the final H_(m-1).
The decomposition is staggered by each actual own checkpoint, not a
simultaneous array of checkpoint margins.

First direct attempt: use the explicit negative mixed margin(27) to
dominate the terminal support in(29). This requires control of the
actual J_i^in AND the later signed integral. A small bound on v_i at
t_(i+1) alone is insufficient: r_i contains the full J_i^in.
The terminal-gradient coefficients p_N,q_N depend on the true reached
state and cannot be freely optimized independently of those histories.

Second direct attempt: telescope the static successor terms across
middle components. If p_i=P_i(n), q_i=Q_i(n), the two neighboring
static D terms in the sum are beta D_(i+2)[q_i-p_(i+1)].
This is -beta D_(i+2) times the ACTUAL combined terminal derivative
at i+1. That derivative is not nonnegative: on a feasible edge-only
terminal support q_i>0 forces U_(i+1)=0 and may have p_(i+1)=0.
Thus the static sum is not a nonpositive boundary term. All staggered
J_i^in terms and all later signed pairings must remain to pay it.
No already-refuted universal cross-time positivity is proposed.

A precise coefficient screen illustrates this failure without making
an initialized counterexample. A feasible pure-edge terminal projection
for component i has p_N=beta q_N, q_N=sigma_i; other retired projections
can be zero. To specify all coordinates, take x_i=A_i,
x_(i+1)=ell_(i+1)-kappa sigma_i, x_(i+2)=ell_(i+2),
then set x_j=min(A_j,ell_j+x_(j+1)/beta)-sigma_j backwards for
j=i-1,...,1, enforcing also x_1<=A_1-beta D_2. Set all remaining
retired coordinates after i+2 to0 and the isolated scalar to
ell_(k+1)+D_(k+1). The zero-cone inequalities show every other retired
gradient vanishes. This defines a feasible single-sample support, not
a chronological history from e_1. Its static coefficient in(29) is

    sigma_i[beta D_(i+2)-beta^2 D_(i+1)-ell_(i+2)/beta]>0     (30)

eventually by the joint amplitude/translation limits. Its negative
gradient norm is only of order sigma_i^2. This refutes the shortcut
that the paid static coefficients alone are nonpositive for every
feasible terminal support. The associated J_i^in and later integral
are NOT claimed zero or reachable with that support. They may cancel
(30), and that actual initialized comparison remains unproved.


## Appendix A: unchanged frozen local geometry and scales

## 1. Exact static function, body and physical word

For each integer m>=16 put

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), k=floor(sqrt(m)), J=m-k, beta=1/k,
    eta_word=1-1/(4m), c=lambda*(1+1/(4m)), a_word=eta_word/c,
    d=c-2,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,b_j,H_j), b_j=1+a_word*c^j,
    S_j=sum H_j, n=2^m-1.

Every child uses this SAME enclosing m and these same parameters. Select
the first occurrences of levels J,...,m-1. Checkpoint i has level
l_i=J+i-1, physical step index 2^l_i-1 and value b_l_i.
The ordinary block masses are s_1=S_J, s_i=S_(J+i-2) for 2<=i<=k,
and the last mass is S=S_(m-1). Every entry of the word is executed.

For 0<=q<=1 define

    p(q)=beta+sqrt((1+beta)^2-(q+beta)^2),
    C=beta+sqrt(1+2beta), e=C-beta=sqrt(1+2beta),
    K=conv({0} union {(p(q),-q):0<=q<=1}),
    kappa=1+beta^2.

The complete horizontal sections of this full body are

    0<=q<=1, beta*q<=p<=p(q).                              (1)

It has the two radial segments to (C,0) and (beta,-1), and the whole
circular arc between them. No finite-plane replacement is made.

For a compact convex body B containing0 put

    E_B(w)=max_(g in B){<g,w>-||g||^2/2}.

Write Hub_L for the one-sided Huber: zero for r<=0, r^2/2 for
0<=r<=L, and L*r-L^2/2 for r>=L.

The local data of every ordinary block are defined using its actual order.
If its physical entries are a_1,...,a_N, set alpha_j=a_j/2.
Start (u_N,-y_N)=(beta,-1), q_N=1, and define backwards

    (q_(j-1)+beta)/(p(q_(j-1))-beta)
       =max{beta/e, (y_j+(1-alpha_j)*beta)/
                              (u_j-(1-alpha_j)*beta)},
    (u_(j-1),-y_(j-1))=(u_j,-y_j)+alpha_j*(p(q_(j-1)),-q_(j-1)).
                                                               (2)

The left side increases continuously from beta/e to infinity. Its right
denominator is positive since u_j>=beta and alpha_j>0. The starting
coordinates for block i are denoted u_i,y_i; set M_i=u_i+y_i/beta.
These are finite symbolic definitions, not a recurrence executed this round.

Fix ell_1=0,D_1=1 and define, before the run,

    T_i=b_l_i/2-1, sigma_i=D_i/M_i,
    ell_(i+1)=sigma_i*y_i, A_i=ell_i+ell_(i+1)/beta,
    D_(i+1)=sigma_i*T_i,
    Phi_i(X,Y)=E_(sigma_i K)(X-A_i,Y-ell_(i+1)),
    delta=D_(k+1)/(1+S),
    F(x)=1/2 sum_(i=1)^k Phi_i(x_i,x_(i+1))
                      +1/2 Hub_delta(x_(k+1)-ell_(k+1)),
    x_0=e_1 in R^(k+1).                                    (3)

We use the range in which every T_i>0; this holds for all sufficiently
large m. This is exactly the frozen circular half-sum.

## 2. Geometry, true radius, and every neighboring gradient

Completing the square in E_B identifies its unique maximizer as Pi_B(w),
the Euclidean projection. The maximizing inequalities at w and w' imply

    ||Pi_B(w)-Pi_B(w')||^2
                <=<Pi_B(w)-Pi_B(w'),w-w'>.

The maximizing formula and this inequality give differentiability with
gradient Pi_B and a 1-Lipschitz gradient. Thus E_B is convex, nonnegative
and1-smooth. Its zero set is the polar cone of B: if some support pairing
is positive, taking a sufficiently small positive multiple of that
support vector gives a positive envelope value.

The cone of K has extreme rays (C,0),(beta,-1). Consequently

    Phi_i=0 iff x_i<=A_i and
                      x_(i+1)>=beta*(x_i-ell_i).            (4)

The disk with center(beta,beta), radius1+beta supports the whole arc.
Its normal and the horizontal endpoint normal prove, in (2),

    Pi_K(u_j,-y_j)=(p(q_j),-q_j),
    u_i=beta+sum alpha_j*p(q_(j-1)),
    y_i=1+sum alpha_j*q_(j-1), u_i>=beta*y_i.                (5)

For the uncapped points the normal ratio is (y_j+beta)/(u_j-beta).
The defining predecessor ratio reduces its numerator by alpha_j*beta
and increases its denominator by the same amount, so q_(j-1)<=q_j.
At the capped endpoint the inequality y<=beta*(u-C)/e is preserved
by every further backward capped step. These facts check the normals
also at the transition and endpoints.

At any global query, define ALL gradients by

    (P_i,-Q_i)=Pi_(sigma_i K)(x_i-A_i,x_(i+1)-ell_(i+1)), i<=k,
    P_(k+1)=clip(x_(k+1)-ell_(k+1),0,delta), Q_0=0.

Every physical step is exactly

    x_j^+=x_j-(h/2)(P_j-Q_(j-1)), 1<=j<=k+1.               (6)

In particular earlier components are never reset or deleted.

If x_(i+1)>=ell_(i+1), replacing (P,-Q) by (P,0) improves its
maximization by Q*(x_(i+1)-ell_(i+1))+Q^2/2 when Q>0.
Thus Q_i=0 in that region. If x_i<=ell_i and its output is
nonnegative, (4) gives Phi_i=0.

These properties prove the active-pair chronology inductively. At block i
the active local states are (A_i+sigma_i*u_j,ell_(i+1)-sigma_i*y_j);
its input is >=ell_i, so Q_(i-1)=0 at the ACTIVE pair's queries.
Its next component vanishes, as do all still-future components.
Earlier components retain (6). The selected step produces, exactly,

    x_i(t_i)=A_i-beta*D_(i+1),
    x_(i+1)(t_i)=ell_(i+1)+D_(i+1),
    t_i=2^l_i.                                             (7)

Equation(7) specifies only that pair at its OWN checkpoint time.
It is not a simultaneous snapshot of the later retired chain.

The map stacking all adjacent pairs and the last scalar counts each
coordinate at most twice. Its squared norm is <=2. Thus the factor1/2
makes F convex1-smooth. F>=0 and F(0)=0. Its entire minimum set is

    C_min={x: x_i<=A_i,
                x_(i+1)>=beta*(x_i-ell_i), 1<=i<=k;
                                      x_(k+1)<=ell_(k+1)}.

The point (A_1,ell_2,0,...,0) belongs to it. From (5),

    1-A_1=sigma_1*u_1, ell_2=beta*A_1=sigma_1*y_1,
    A_1<=1/(1+beta^2).

For x_1<0 the squared distance from e_1 is at least1.
For 0<=x_1<=A_1 it is at least (1-x_1)^2+beta^2*x_1^2,
decreasing on this interval and attained by that feasible point. Therefore

    R_m^2=dist(e_1,C_min)^2
          =(1-A_1)^2+ell_2^2=(u_1^2+y_1^2)/M_1^2>0.        (8)

All physical samples, terminal samples and minimizers obey every ordered
smooth-convex interpolation inequality. To check this directly, apply
the 1-smooth descent inequality to F(z)-<grad F(y),z>, minimized at y;
it gives F(x)-F(y)-<grad F(y),x-y>>=||grad F(x)-grad F(y)||^2/2.
The Gram matrices are actual Euclidean Gram matrices. Scaling F by an
advertised L>0 and using steps h/L preserves (6) and the normalized payoff.

## 3. Uniform scales and the strictly scoped accepted input

Here are the bounds needed for the normalizations, derived jointly so no
earlier unverified estimate is used as a premise.

In a backward block set w_j=(y_j+beta)/(q_j+beta). Expansion of (2),
using the circular normal at uncapped points, gives

    w_(j-1)/w_j
       =1+[p(q_(j-1))*q_j-q_(j-1)*p(q_j)]/
                         [beta*(p(q_(j-1))+q_(j-1))].       (9)

At a transition to q_(j-1)=0 this follows from y_(j-1)=y_j;
if both q's are zero it is equality. The numerator is the integral
of p(q_(j-1))-q_(j-1)*p'(t). After division by p(q_(j-1))+q_(j-1)
the integrand is <=max(1,-p'(t)). Since w_N=1, multiplication gives

    y_i<=Y_k:=2 exp(2k),
    integral_0^1 max(1,-p'(t))dt=sqrt(2)*(1+beta)-beta<2.

For the integral split at t=(1+beta)/sqrt(2)-beta.
At uncapped points u<=beta+2k*(Y_k+beta). The uncapped backward steps
are contiguous and have p>=beta; their combined deficit from C times
half their mass is <=4k^2*(Y_k+beta). The remaining p's equal C.
Paying also y_i/beta and beta gives

    |M_i-C*s_i/2|<=K_k:=20k^2 exp(2k).                     (10)

The concave function p(q)+q/beta has smaller endpoint C. Using (5),

    M_i>=beta+1/beta+C*s_i/2>=C*(1+s_i/2).                 (11)

Let v_mass=a_word/d. Since c*(c-2)>1>eta_word, 0<v_mass<1.
Substitution in the exact mass relation gives

    1+S_j=v_mass*c^j+(1-v_mass)*2^j,
    b_j=d*S_j+c-1-d*(1-v_mass)*2^j.                       (12)

Uniformly for J<=j<=m-1 the relative leading-mass error is
O(c^(-J)+(2/c)^J). Also k*K_k/S_J->0: its numerator has logarithm
O(k+log k) and the denominator has logarithm J*log(lambda)+O(1).
Using 0<=x-log(1+x)<=x^2/2 with x=1/(4m) yields

    S_J/lambda^J->exp(1/4),
    S_(m-r)/lambda^(m-r)->exp(1/4) for every fixed r>=1.

The first amplitude ratio T_1/M_1 is (d/C)*(1+o(1)); the other amplitude
ratios are (c*d/C)*(1+o(1)). The individual relative errors are uniformly
O(K_k/S_J+c^(-J)+(2/c)^J), and k times this bound tends to zero.
Taking logarithms in the product therefore proves

    D_(k+1)=(d/C)*(c*d/C)^(k-1)*(1+o(1))
                 ->(lambda-2)*exp(-2),
    R_m^2->1, sigma_1~2/(C*S_J),
    sigma_(i+1)/sigma_i=T_i/M_(i+1)->lambda-2               (13)

uniformly for i<k. Here c*d=1+O(1/m) and log(C)/beta->2,
so C^k->exp(2). Eventually every adjacent sigma ratio is in [1/3,1/2],
and sum sigma_i^2<=2sigma_1^2.

The ORIGINAL ALL-SPLIT benchmark is C_0^bench=B_0=1,

    C_n^bench=max_(0<=r<n)Omega(C_r^bench,C_(n-r-1)^bench),
    B_n=max_(0<=r<n)G(C_r^bench,B_(n-r-1)),
    Omega(a,b)=(a+b+sqrt((a+b)^2+4ab))/2,
    G(a,b)=2a+b/2+sqrt(b^2+8ab)/2, U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.

The circle constant C in this report is distinct from C_n^bench.
Only the unconditional ray conclusion of accepted fact04219554e41d9750
is used:

    0<Z_1=lim_m lambda^(-m) B_(2^m-1)<infinity,
    Z_1<5.                                                (14)

The latter follows from its b=1,j=0 upper enclosure, B_1=4, and
(2+3/100)^2<5. No seed hypothesis is assumed.

Put q_root=t_k=2^(m-1), N=2^(m-1)-1. The final suffix is the actual
ordered H_(m-1), indexed t=0,...,N. During this suffix the last excess
is D_(k+1)-delta times half the mass already read, and stays >=delta.
Output isolation gives Q_k=0, proving this claim by induction.
Thus its terminal Huber numerator is exactly D_(k+1)^2/[2(1+S)].

The retired function and its exact updates are

    G_ret(x)=1/2 sum_(i<k)Phi_i(x_i,x_(i+1))
                         +1/2 Hub_(C*sigma_k)(x_k-A_k),
    d_i=P_i-Q_(i-1), x^+=x-(h/2)d, Q_0=Q_k=0,
    V_m=2G_ret(x(N))=sum_(i=1)^k Phi_i(x(N)).

Phi_k denotes the original last circular value, exactly equal to this
horizontal Huber at these actual states. Hence

    B_n*2F(x_n)/R_m^2=T_m+B_n*V_m/R_m^2,
    T_m=B_n*D_(k+1)^2/[2(1+S)*R_m^2]
                  ->T_infty=Z_1*exp(-17/4)/(2lambda)<5/64. (15)

The useful finite-error normalizing scale is

    (B_n/R_m^2)*sigma_1^2*(1+K_k+2^m)->0.                  (16)

Indeed B_n*sigma_1^2=O(lambda^(-m+2k)); multiplying by K_k
still decays exponentially in m. The remaining term is
O((2/lambda)^m*lambda^(2k)), also tending to zero. No numerical
evaluation, finite benchmark replay or m>=1536 inference is used.
